## Document 010: 2026-09-25_［日銀短観予測］堅調なAI関連需要を受け、製造業の景況感が改善すると予想_72bbfa5b

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-09-25_［日銀短観予測］堅調なAI関連需要を受け、製造業の景況感が改善すると予想_72bbfa5b.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-09-25
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

堅調なAI関連需要を受け、製造 
業の景況感が改善すると予想

○ 業況判断ＤＩ予測：大企業・製造業   ＋25％Pt（6月調査から3ポイント改善） 
                  大企業・非製造業 ＋37％Pt（6月調査から横ばい） 
○ 2026年度設備投資計画(全規模・全産業)予測：前年度比＋7.8％（6月調査：同＋6.8％）

大企業・製造業の業況判断は
改善、大企業・非製造業の業
況判断は横ばいを予想 
10月1日公表の日銀短観（9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況判断DI
が＋25％Ptと、6月調査（＋22％Pt）から3ポイントの改善を予想（図表1）
する。関連指標では、7～9月期法人企業景気予測調査において、大企業の
「貴社の景況判断BSI」は製造業を中心に改善したほか、9月ロイター短観で
は、半導体やデータセンター（DC）関連の受注が好調との声が目立った。AI
関連需要が旺盛ななか、生産用機械や化学、非鉄金属などの関連業種で幅広
く景況感が改善すると見込む。 
図表１ 業況判断DIの予測

（注）2026 年9 月調査はみずほ総合研究所調査部による予測値

### Page 2

2

実際、AI関連財輸出の増勢は拡大している（図表2） 。半導体・電子部品の
輸出は、メモリ価格上昇の恩恵を受けて前年比で大幅プラスとなっているほ
か、足元では半導体製造・検査機器の輸出数量が急増している。その他、DC
に使われる冷却装置などの設備や、半導体製造に不可欠な原材料・素材の輸
出も増加するなど、輸出品目の裾野は広がっているようだ。こうしたもと、
AI関連財の生産指数は2025年9月比＋11.3％と大幅に上昇（図表3）してお
り、AI関連財の増産が鉱工業生産全体のけん引役になっている。 
また、足元にかけて円安基調が継続していることも、こうした輸出志向の
強い製造業種の景況感を下支えしている。 
大企業・非製造業の業況判断DIは＋37％Pt（6月調査：＋37％Pt）と、高
水準が継続する見込みだ。 
既往の資源高を背景に、卸売業の景況感が堅調となることが期待されるほ
か、堅調な投資需要を背景に、建設業でも良好な景況感が続きそうだ。さら
に、不動産価格や賃料の上昇を受けて、不動産業も好調を維持するとみてい
る。 
宿泊・飲食サービス等のインバウンド関連業種の景況感も底堅い結果が予
想される。訪日外客数については、短期的にはやや増勢が鈍化している。中
東情勢悪化を背景とした航空便の減便や、燃油サーチャージの上昇を受けた
航空運賃の負担増などが背景にある。日中対立を受けて中国の訪日外客数が
大幅減となっている状況も不変だ。もっとも、韓国や台湾など近隣諸国から
の訪日外客数は大幅な増加が続いており、全体としてみれば、大幅な腰折れ
は回避されている。 
図表２ 日本のAI関連財輸出額 図表３ 鉱工業生産

を表示

### Page 3

3

なお、製造業・非製造業に共通する要因として、中東情勢悪化を受けた石
化製品価格など原材料価格の上昇や人件費上昇といったコスト増による業績
下押し圧力は、日本企業全体としてみれば限定的なものに留まるとみてい
る。大企業やサプライチェーンの上流企業を中心に価格転嫁が進んでおり、
コスト増を一定程度吸収できている。上述の法人企業景気予測調査における
2026年度の経常利益計画が製造業・非製造業ともに上方修正され、増益見込
みに転じていることも、全体としての企業景況感が底堅さを維持するとみて
いる根拠の一つである。ただし、中小企業や、消費者に近いサプライチェー
ンの下流企業では十分な価格転嫁が進まず、景況感の下押し圧力となること
が予想される。 
先行きの業況判断DIは、製造業・非製造業ともに企業規模を問わず悪化が
見込まれる。もっとも、先行きの業況判断DIは現在対比で下振れしやすいク
セのある統計である。中東情勢が引き続き予断を許さないことなど、懸念材
料はあるが、実態としては、日本企業は先行きの景気をさほど悲観的に見て
いない可能性が高い。 
2026年度も、引き続き旺盛な
投資計画が示される見通し 
2026年度の設備投資計画 （全規模 ・ 全産業） は同＋7.8％（6月調査：＋6.8％）
と上方修正を予測する。従来からの旺盛な投資需要に加え、政府が7月以降開
始した「大胆な投資促進税制」に基づく投資支援策も、民間企業の投資需要
を下支えする公算が大きい。中東情勢悪化に伴う不確実性が前回調査時点か
ら幾分後退しているとみられることも、ポジティブな材料だ。 
日本政策投資銀行が公表した 「全国設備投資計画調査（2026年6月）」に よ
れば、化学・電気機械・非鉄金属・電力など、幅広い業種で旺盛なAI関連需
要に対応することを目的とした投資が計画されており、投資のけん引役とな
ることが期待される。 
不動産やサービス業では、ホテル開発や娯楽施設の高付加価値化といった
インバウンド関連投資も好調のようだ。自動車では、EV関連投資が一部減退
し、2025年度計画比では控えめな計画になることが予想されるものの、全体
としてみれば旺盛な投資需要が示されるだろう。

市場焦點
美國
• 7月個人所得月增 0.4%、可支配所得月增 0.5%，均較前月回升，反映勞動市場與家庭收入仍
具韌性，然而個人消費支出僅月增 0.2%，實質PCE近乎持平，且儲蓄率由 6月的2.7%升至
3.0%，顯示部分新增所得轉向儲蓄 ，消費動能趨於溫和；而 7月PCE年增率維持 3.7%，核心
PCE年增3.3%，明顯高於Fed通膨目標，凸顯通膨降溫進程仍顯緩慢。整體而言，美國經濟呈
現收入成長、消費放緩及通膨黏性並存的局面，後續成長與政策走向仍將牽動市場情緒。
AI
• 輝達最新一季營收達 962億美元，創歷史新高，其中資料中心營收達 890億美元，占總營收近
93%，年增117%，為主要成長引擎 。展望第三季，公司預估營收將達 1,080億美元，季增
12%，且預測尚未納入中國資料中心運算收入 ，顯示超大規模雲端服務商及 AI業者仍持續擴

充算力。部分科技巨頭相對標普500指數的評價已降至過去十年區間相對低檔，在AI需求持續
成長及企業獲利維持強勁的背景下，後續仍具估值修復空間。
黃金
• 8月推升金價反彈的主要因素來自 ETF逢低買進與美國財政擔憂的「貶值交易」，不過上週五
聯準會新任主席華許在全球央行年會上發表鷹派立場，金價8月以來的漲幅略有收斂。
• 短線來看，聯準會基準利率的前景仍會為金價帶來波動，在較高利率水準的環境下，資金(如
ETF買盤)願意持續加碼的意願可能有所受限。待市場消化完基本面消息後，短線金價可能再度
隨著通膨及利率前景的變化而波動。

顧
當前景氣評價改善、未來預期轉弱，消費動能面臨考驗
• 消費者信心降至 7個月低點，未來預期明顯轉弱：美國8月
諮商會消費者信心指數降至 89.4，低於市場預期，創7個月
新低；其中預期指數大幅下滑 5.8點至68.2，跌破80的歷史
警戒線，顯示消費者對未來景氣 、就業及家庭所得前景轉
趨保守。另一方面，汽油及食品價格仍偏高 ，持續影響家
庭對生活成本的感受，使短期消費動能面臨壓力。
• 現況評價改善，消費韌性未明顯轉弱：值得注意的是，8月
現況指數反向上升 6.8點至121.2，為連續三個月下滑後首
度回升；就業市場差額指標亦改善 ，顯示消費者對當前經
濟及勞動市場的評價仍具韌性 。因此，本次信心下滑較偏
向「對未來的擔憂升高」，但若預期指數持續低於 80，後
續仍須留意家庭支出與企業營收成長的潛在壓力。

• 後市觀察：消費信心轉弱，加上油價偏高、通膨黏性及就
業前景不確定，可能持續抑制非必要消費。另一方面，7月
新屋銷售下滑10.5%，顯示高利率環境仍對房市與利率敏感
型產業形成壓力。建議投資人可優先布局大型科技 、半導
體，以及受惠 AI發展與電力需求成長的公用事業及基礎建
設類股。若後續通膨持續降溫並帶動利率下行 ，則可逐步
增加消費及房地產相關類股配置。

收入增長支撐家庭財務、消費動能放緩，通膨韌性限制降息空間
• 所得增長優於消費 ，家庭支出轉趨審慎：美國7月個人
所得月增 0.4%、可支配所得 月增0.5%，均較 6月的
0.2%改善；但個人消費支出僅增 0.2%，實質PCE幾乎
持平，顯示所得成長未明顯轉化為消費動能，家庭支出
態度轉趨審慎；儲蓄率則由6月2.7%升至3.0%，反映家
庭可能將部分新增所得轉為儲蓄。
• 通膨仍具黏著性，Fed進一步寬鬆空間：7月PCE價格指
數月增 0.2%、年增 3.7%，核心 PCE月增 0.2%、年增
3.3%，明顯高於 Fed2%通膨目標；在實質消費放緩的
同時，通膨降溫速度仍不足，使Fed須在支持經濟與抑
制通膨間取得平衡，後續政策走向仍取決於通膨與就業
數據。
• 投資策略建議：通膨尚未回到目標、利率走向仍具不確

定性的環境下，短期建議採股債平衡、偏防禦配置，債
券可優先布局短中天期的美國投資級公司債，在利率仍
具不確定性下兼顧收益與利率風險；股票則可關注高股
息、現金流穩健及防禦型產業，降低對景氣循環與高估
值成長股的曝險。若後續核心PCE持續回落，才有利於
延長債券存續期間並增加成長型資產配置。

企業獲利表現強勁，科技與金融仍是市場核心動能
• 企業獲利表現強勁，科技與金融仍是市場核心動能：美股財報季進入尾聲，整體企業獲利表現優於預期，標普500成分股中
約86%企業獲利優於市場預期，整體獲利年增率達50.4%，創2021年第2季以來最佳單季表現。從產業別觀察，11大產業中
僅醫療類股獲利衰退，能源、金融及科技則為獲利預期上修幅度最大的板塊。值得注意的是，科技、金融與工業三大板塊近
三個月亦大舉推進股票回購，展現企業對自身營運的信心與企業現金流充裕，提供基本面支撐。
• 科技股宜採長期布局，定期定額降低波動影響：科技產業雖具較高波動度，但同時也是創新與長期成長動能最強的領域。對
於長期投資人而言，透過定期定額方式分散進場時點，可降低短期市場波動對投資成本的影響，並參與科技產業長期成長趨

勢。統計1971年2月至2026年7月任意期間投資那斯達克指數3年，平均報酬率接近21%，且正報酬機率超過8成，顯示面對
科技股短期波動，採取「定期定額、長期持有」策略，有助於提升投資勝率並掌握長期複利效果。
標普500指數各產業獲利成長 標普500指數各類近三個月宣布的股票回購計劃

債券比重下滑的三大原因—防禦失靈、現金崛起與股市磁吸
• 過去債券一直被譽為投資組合避風港 ，然而，彭博研究最新數據卻顯示債券 ETF佔全球ETF總資產比重已大幅下滑至約 16%，
創自2007年以來最低紀錄，相比疫情期間接近23%高峰，債券資產正在被市場邊緣化，三大原因如下：
• 股債負相關失效，減震效果不佳：過去幾十年，當股市暴跌時，債券通常會上漲，進而幫投資者減震。但數據顯示(以美國
綜合債券ETF-AGG為例)，在美股(SPY)下跌的日子裡，AGG同時上漲的機率已顯著下降。當債券無法在股市下跌時提供保
護，投資人持有傳統債券的核心理由便不復存在。
• 貨幣市場基金與超短期公債(現金類資產)強勢崛起：在高利率環境下，投資人發現持有現金比持有中長期債券更有吸引力。
貨幣市場基金與超短期公債ETF能提供與傳統債券相當甚至更好的收益率，卻低存續期間風險且流動性極佳。目前貨幣市場

基金佔基金資產比重已達24%，躍居成為市場最青睞的核心資產。
• 強勁股市與資本追求成長：在股市持續創高的牛市行情下，投資人更傾向將資金注入股票以追求高成長 (共同基金中股票佔
比高達77%創歷史新高)，或是將避險資金放在貨幣市場。
債券ETF占總資產的% AGG上漲/SPY下跌天數
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.8.28
貨幣市場基金 vs. 傳統債券基金資產

發行機構的市場轉型—被動指數退場，主動管理與靈活策略崛起
• 市場的需求直接影響金融產品供給端，ETF發行機構的動作傳遞出強烈的轉型訊號。
• 債券新產品發行量落入歷史低點：今年發行的新 ETF中，債券型僅佔約 13.8%，發行機構對推出傳統債券 ETF興趣大不如前。
• 主動型債券ETF成為主流(近60%)：在今年少數發行的債券 ETF中，近六成採用主動管理策略，而非追蹤傳統的綜合債券指
數。資產管理業者意識到，面對複雜的利率環境，投資人需要的是能夠靈活調整存續期 (久期)、精準管理信用風險並爭取超
額回報的主動型經理人，而非死板的被動追蹤指數。
• 小結及建議：在股市當前債券ETF佔比回落至2007年低點，並不意味著固定收益資產將完全消失，而是市場對債券角色進行
重新定義。傳統被動型、長存續期債券作為避險工具的效果大打折扣，資金正向極短期的現金類資產與主動靈活的債券策略

兩極化分流。針對不同需求的投資人，以下三點資產配置策略可供參考：
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.8.28
債券ETF發行趨勢1. 以現金/超短期國債替換部分傳統中長期債券：若您的目標是尋求資產組合的
穩定與流動性，建議將部分傳統債券配置移至貨幣市場基金或超短期美國國債 ，
能在鎖定高利的同時，免於承受利率升降導致的債券價格劇烈波動。
2. 放棄傳統被動債券，轉向主動型債券策略：
若仍需要固定收益來提供定期現金流，請避免盲目購買被動追蹤大盤指數的傳
統債券ETF，應優先考慮主動管理型債券ETF或基金，利用經理人的專業操作，
動態調整信用等級與存續期，以應對當前高波動的利率環境。
3. 重新評估資產組合的風險承受度：
目前許多投資人股票配置過於集中 (如共同基金股票佔比達 77%)，請檢視自身
組合，切勿因股市上漲而盲目追高；可將股票獲利落袋部分 ，靈活分配於高殖
利率超短期國債與優質主動債券型ETF或基金，建構更具韌性的投資組合。

中國政府債券融資占⽐逼近人民幣貸款
• 中國政府債券融資占⽐逼近人民幣貸款 ：過去12個月的資金流來看，中國政府債券占社會融資規模增量的⽐例自 2024年7月
起超過30%，今年7月達43%，而實體經濟發放的人⺠幣貸款占⽐為45%，這反映出融資渠道從間接融資(尤其是人⺠幣貸款)
轉向政府債券的改變，彭博預估此趨勢將會持續，因為中國官方有意在傳統銀行貸款之外轉向更依賴政府債券，並在貨幣政
策框架中，降低對貨幣供應量的重視程度，此趨勢轉變可能表示中國第四季將更依靠財政刺激來支持成長 (重點促進政府債
券需求和發行)，而⾮大幅下調政策利率或存款準備金率。
• 展望後市：第四季中國利率下行的空間有限，結構性貨幣政策工具和財政支持，仍是推動經濟成長的主要⼿段，預計中國央
行將繼續推進利率政策框架改⾰，以及債券市場國際化進程，與此同時更多政府債券的發行，以及債券估值水平的調整，可

能推動殖利率走高。

貶值交易+資金行情推升近一個月金價，短線或震盪整理
• 8月以來金價走揚：8月推升金價反彈的主要因素來自 ETF逢
低買進與美國財政擔憂的「貶值交易」，不過上週五聯準會
新任主席華許在全球央行年會上發表鷹派立場，金價8月以來
的漲幅略有收斂。
• 「貶值交易」拖累美元匯價 ，進而支撐金價：美國財政部公
布截至8/18未償公共債務總額已正式升至 40兆美元，持有的
聯邦債務約占國內生產毛額 (GDP)的100%，逼近二次世界大
戰後的歷史紀錄，儘管隨後公布擴大公債回購規模 ，仍難以
扭轉市場對於債務問題的擔憂 ，美元信心下降、美債遭到拋
售令美元指數走弱，進而推升具資產保值特性的黃金價格。
• 觀點：除了債務問題，地緣政治風險也使美元信心下降 ，有
望為黃金價格帶來中長期的支撐 。短線來看，聯準會基準利

率的前景仍會為金價帶來波動 ，在較高利率水準的環境下，
資金(如ETF買盤)願意持續加碼的意願可能有所受限。待市場
消化完基本面消息後，短線金價可能再度隨著通膨及利率前
景的變化而波動，建議以資產配置的角度，逢回布局。 資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.8.27。
8月以來資金顯著流入黃金金融商品
留意短線金價波動可能放大

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支

績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

至 4.00%，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息。委員會認為美國經濟及勞動

最高 100%關稅。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.9.21 
一、 美國—消費回暖、房市降溫，Fed 政策轉向與川習會成市場焦點 
2026 年 8 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,739 億美元，較
上月增加 1.2%，優於 7 月修正後月減 0.5%的表現，年增率亦達 6.0%，顯示美國
消費支出動能於 8 月明顯回升。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增1.4%、年增
6.9%；進一步扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增1.2%、年增 5.6%，
反映核心消費需求維持穩健成長。從主要業別表現觀察，非實體零售商銷售額月
增 2.6%、加油站月增 3.1%、電子及家電商店月增 1.6%，食品服務及飲酒場所亦
月增 1.2%，顯示線上消費、能源相關支出及服務性消費持續成長；另一方面，建
築材料與園藝用品經銷商月減 0.2%，對整體零售表現形成部分抵銷。整體而言，
8 月美國零售銷售由前月下滑轉為明顯成長，且扣除汽車及能源相關項目後仍維
持穩健增幅，顯示美國消費需求仍具支撐力道，對經濟活動形成正面挹注，惟後
續仍須持續關注物價變動、金融情勢及消費信心對零售支出的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國住宅市場持續呈現分化走勢，建築許可年化總數降至 139.4
萬戶，月減 2.7%，惟較去年同期增加 3.5%；其中單戶住宅建築許可 87.8 萬戶，
月減 1.8%，5 戶以上多戶住宅建築許可 46.7 萬戶，月減 3.1%，顯示住宅開發規
劃動能較前月略有降溫 。 另一方面 ， 新屋開工年化數降至127.5 萬戶 ， 月減2.6%、
年減 1.2%，其中單戶住宅開工回升至 91.8 萬戶，月增 7.6%、年增 5.2%，為近 5
個月最高水準 ； 惟5 戶以上多戶住宅開工降至 34.4 萬戶 ， 月減22.5%、 年減15.5%，
拖累整體開工表現。施工方面，在建住宅年化數為 127.1 萬戶，月增 0.3%，但較
去年同期減少 3.2%，顯示住宅建設規模仍呈收斂趨勢；完工年化數則降至 112.8
萬戶，月減 11.9%、年減 27.1%，反映住宅供給落成速度明顯放緩。整體而言，8
月美國住宅市場呈現單戶住宅需求相對具支撐、但多戶住宅及整體營建活動偏弱
的格局，顯示在高利率與需求前景仍具不確定性的環境下，建商投資態度仍趨審
慎，後續仍須關注利率走勢及房市需求變化對住宅市場的影響。 
2026 年 9 月聯邦公開市場委員會(FOMC)會議決議以 12 比 0 一致同意將聯邦
基金利率目標區間調升 1 碼至 3.75%至 4.00%，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息，謹

下：(一)儘管地緣政治風險推升經濟前景不確定性，美國經濟活動仍維持穩健擴

也不預設利率路徑，將依經濟數據與展望機動調整政策。 
在 9 月 24 日美國華府白宮舉行之 「川習會」 前 ， 美國財長貝森特(Scott Bessent)
與中國國務院副總理何立峰於紐約摩根大通總部舉行會談，美國貿易代表葛里爾
(Jamieson Greer) 亦共同參與。雙方將就人工智慧 (AI)、關稅、貿易休戰及關鍵礦
產供應等議題交換意見，並為川普與習近平會晤預作準備。討論重點包括將於11
月 10 日到期之美中貿易休戰安排、中國稀土磁材及關鍵礦產出口供應情況，以
及 AI 模型安全與治理機制等議題。此外，雙方亦將檢視今年 5 月北京會談後尚
待落實之事項，包括降低部分非戰略性商品關稅、中國增加美國農產品採購及波
音客機採購計畫等。市場普遍預期本次會談將以維持既有合作架構及避免貿易緊
張升級為主要目標，短期內出現重大突破的可能性仍相對有限，美中關稅、關鍵
礦產供應及 AI 治理議題預料將成為本次川習會的重要焦點。 
美國政府近期在美墨加協定(USMCA)檢討談判中，要求墨西哥針對人工智慧
(AI)伺服器、半導體及相關硬體產品建立更嚴格的原產地規範，擬限制來自中國
等非北美地區零組件占比，並可能參照汽車產業的區域含量標準，以防堵外國企
業透過墨西哥組裝後出口美國，規避美國對中國商品實施的關稅與貿易限制。市
場分析指出，在美國 AI 基礎建設需求帶動下，墨西哥已成為重要 AI 伺服器生產

基地，今年上半年 AI 資料中心伺服器出口金額達 830 億美元，其中 94%銷往美
國，並超越汽車成為對美最大出口項目。由於鴻海等台灣電子代工廠在墨西哥設
有 AI 伺服器生產據點，若相關規範正式上路，供應鏈可能被迫提高北美在地採
購及生產比重，進一步重塑北美 AI 硬體供應鏈布局。美墨雙方預計 9 月底於華
府展開新一輪談判，惟相關原產地門檻、過渡期間及適用範圍尚未定案。 
美國總統川普已於 9 月 18 日簽署《2026 年 Lindsey O. Graham 制裁俄羅斯與
伊朗法案》，該法案此前分別以參議院 86 票對 11 票、眾議院 262 票對 159 票通
過。法案除擴大對俄羅斯政府官員、金融機構及協助能源運輸的「影子艦隊」制
裁外，亦授權總統對持續大量購買俄羅斯石油及天然氣的第三國商品課徵最高
100%關稅，其中中國、印度等俄羅斯能源主要買家可能受到影響。法案目的在於
削弱俄羅斯能源出口收入及其支撐烏俄戰爭的財政來源，並強化對伊朗的相關制
裁。 
二、歐元區—美歐矛盾與歐中貿易協商在即，增添工業復甦變數 
 歐元區 7 月工業生產較 6 月下滑 0.1%，為連續兩個月衰退，其中非耐久消費
財產出減少 1.6%，抵銷中間財、資本財、消費耐久財分別成長0.3%、0.5%、0.9%
的影響，主要是美伊衝突帶來的庫存儲備需求持續消退，抵銷德國軍備採購及消
暑用品消費增加的影響。以國別而言，德國工業生產下跌1.5%最為疲軟，且為該
國近一年最大跌幅，主要是部分汽車大廠決定提前進行歲修，故預期待歲修結束
後，8、9 月份可望出現汽車庫存回補的生產需求，此外，在德國軍備採購需求的
支持下 ， 德國7 月工業訂單仍大幅成長 2.5%， 相關產出可望在第三季末及第四季
陸續反映；法、義、西工業生產月增率則分別為-0.4%、0.7%、0.6%。 
 整體而言，美伊衝突爆發後，能源密集產業普遍增產，但該類產業從 6 月起
已有較明顯的趨緩跡象，其他主要產業則大致呈現橫向波動，顯示工業生產趨勢
仍屬溫和。惟近期歐盟提議讓加拿大成為歐盟準會員國，引發美國不滿，可能進
一步升高美歐貿易與外交摩擦；此外，歐盟與中國預定於 10 月 8 日針對貿易失
衡問題進行重要協商 ， 後續若涉及貿易措施調整 ， 亦可能影響歐元區製造業表現 。 
三、日本—央行升息 1 碼，12 月仍有再升息空間 
7 月核心機械訂單月減 3.7%，其中製造業訂單小幅下滑，主要受到化學
產業訂單減少影響，惟電子產品及電氣機械訂單持續成長，反映人工智慧相

關產業及供應鏈企業仍積極擴充產能，支撐資本支出動能。 雖然非製造業訂
單則因資通訊及資訊服務業需求回落影響而表現較弱，但在人力短缺與數位
轉型需求支撐下，投資動能仍具韌性。綜合來看，企業獲利穩健及AI 投資熱
潮延續，預期日本企業資本支出仍將維持成長態勢。 
8 月出口年增 19.3%，半導體相關產品仍為主要成長動能，其中半導體等
電子零件出口大幅成長 52.3%。從地區來看，對美國、中國及歐盟出口分別
成長 24.9%、20.6%及 11.0%，半導體等電子零組件對中國出口金額，以及對
美國 、 歐盟的半導體製造設備出口均較去年同期倍增，顯示全球 AI 及高科技
產業投資需求持續支撐日本出口表現。 
8 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 1.9%，主要受到政府恢復電力與天然
氣補貼，以及米價回落抑制食品價格所致。從細項來看，石油產品價格上漲
帶動家庭消費品等核心商品價格續漲；服務類通膨除通訊費用趨穩外，其他
服務項目已開始出現漲價跡象。展望未來，能源成本上升及企業將 薪資成本
逐步轉嫁至售價，預料將推動通膨壓力由商品擴散至更多服務領域，使通膨
維持緩步升溫趨勢，並進一步支持央行持續推動貨幣政策正常化。 
日本央行於 9 月 17、18 日召開貨幣政策會議，宣布將政策利率調升 1 碼
至 1.25%。雖然政策聲明整體基調變化不大，但仍持續強調通膨上行風險，
並指出目前金融條件仍相對寬鬆。 值得關注的是 ， 本次決策出現兩張反對票 ，
顯示委員會內部對經濟與通膨前景看法分歧加劇，一派認為當前經濟與通膨
動能尚不足以支持升息，另一派則認為通膨升溫風險高於預期。在會後記者
會上，總裁植田和男指出，日本貨幣政策已進入新的階段，由過去較重視通
膨低於目標的風險，轉為兼顧通膨上、下行風險，並強調未來升息步調將視
經濟與物價發展而定，不會預先承諾特定路徑。此外，他也表示 主要國家央
行升息對日本匯率及金融環境具有重要影響，因此將持續納入政策考量。雖
然總裁未透露最終利率水準，但相關談話暗示目前 1.25%的政策利率可能尚
未達到中性水準，日本央行亦未排除未來進一步升息的可能性。整體而言，
日本央行對通膨前景看法趨於樂觀，政策立場仍偏向緊縮，預期若未來數季
通膨持續走揚，日本央行仍有望於 12 月再度升息。

四、中國—8 月內需持續疲乏，與歐美的關係將為近期關注重點 
在內需方面，1-8 月累計零售額年增率較 2025 年同期增加 1.1%，較 1-7 月累
計的 1.2%持續下降。其中 8 月零售額年增率放緩 0.2 個百分點至 0.4%，反映以
舊換新的補貼力道不如過去兩年水準，加上房市低迷，以及對景氣波動較為敏感
的消費耐久財需求進一步下滑 ，8 月家具 、 汽車 、 裝潢消費分別年減7.9%、18.5%、
11.8%。另外，1-8 月固定資產投資年減幅由 6.7%擴大至 7.2%，反映美伊衝突、
政府主導去化過剩產能、美國貿易戰等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎縮產
業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等。同期間房地產開發
投資額年減幅由 19.2%擴大至 19.9%、施工面積年減幅則擴大為 13.2%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動 ， 抵銷內需疲軟的影響 ，8 月工業生產年增率較 7
月回升 0.7 個百分點至 5.2%(1-8 月工業生產年增率持平 1-7 月水準為 5.3%)。依
產業別來看，8 月份高科技產品仍是主要成長動能，包含 IC(成長 20.6%)、工業
機器人(34.6%)、服務機器人(5%)、 新能源車(21.9%)等 ； 惟受到原油供應減少以及
能源價格上漲壓力影響，化纖、水泥、玻璃、鋼材等產量持續衰退，另因 3C 關
鍵物料漲價影響，手機產量衰退幅度高達 22.3%，太陽能電池則因取消出口退稅
政策跌幅高達 12.9%。展望未來，若9 月 24 日川習會有正向發展，美中關稅休戰
得以延長 ， 且歐中貿易摩擦未進一步惡化 ， 將有助於穩定中國出口與製造業景氣 ，
抵銷內需疲軟的經濟壓力。

飲料（5.95%）、交通運輸（4.6%）餐飲和住宿服務（8.38%）及個人照護/社
會安全 （15.17%） 價格亦大幅上漲。儘管通膨水準仍維持在印度央行目標區間
內，惟生產成本居高不下，恐進一步外溢到消費端，並衝擊內需表現。

五、台灣—央行維持利率不變並適度鬆綁不動產信用管制措施 
台灣央行於 9 月 17 日召開理監事會議，決議維持政策利率(重貼現率)於 2%
不變，央行指出，雖然國際油價維持高檔、服務類通膨仍具黏性，但國內通膨仍
屬可控範圍，2026 年 1 至 8 月通膨年增 1.84%，預估 2027 年可回落至 2%以下；
同時考量中東地緣政治、美國經貿政策及 AI 產業發展等不確定因素，因此採取
審慎觀望態度，暫不升息。 
此外，央行認為先前實施之選擇性信用管制已逐漸見效，房市交易降溫、不
動產貸款集中度下降，因此適度調整相關措施，包括將自然人第二戶購屋貸款最
高成數由 6 成提高至 7 成，並刪除購地貸款須切結於一定期間內動工興建之規
定，改由金融機構依授信實務審慎評估辦理。整體而言，本次政策方向呈現利率
維持審慎偏緊基調、房市管制微幅鬆綁。

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,459. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

## Document 090: 261006_daiwa_winway

Source PDF: all report pdf/261006_daiwa_winway.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Winway reported September revenue of TWD1.25bn (-26.7% MoM; +87.7% 
YoY), brining 3Q26 revenue to a record high of TWD4.56bn (+29.3% QoQ; +152.6% YoY). 
The 3Q26 top-line result beat market consensus by 6.7% but missed Daiwa’s estimate by 
9.1%. Following August’s exceptionally strong top line (TWD1.71bn), we had factored in a 
higher shipment run-rate for September. However, the miss stems primarily from the 
lumpiness of probe-card deliveries (high concentration to a single customer) and customer 
qualification timelines, rather than order cancellations or share loss.

We forecast 4Q26 revenue to track flattish to slightly down QoQ, reflecting lower probe-
card shipment volumes compared to 3Q26, alongside typical year-end pull-in deceleration 
from customers. On profitability, we maintain our view that 2Q26 marked the trough for 
gross margin this year. As the revenue contribution from lower-margin MEMS probe cards 
declines, a more favourable product mix should drive a steady QoQ gross margin recovery, 
which may drive gross margin to reach 40% earlier than market expectations.

Although we remain positive on Winway’s structural growth profile, near-term catalysts 
appear muted. This reflects the revenue lumpiness in probe cards, while additional socket 
capacity will not come online until March 2027—pushing the corresponding revenue 
recognition into 2Q27. We will revisit our financial model upon the company’s 3Q26 results 
release. We currently have a Buy (1) rating on Winway with a 12-month TP of TWD10,000.

## Document 140: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 244: Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Thematic)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

### Page 2

— Chúng tôi dự báo vĩ mô trong nước đối với: (1) tỷ giá USD/VND tới cuối năm 2026 đi 
ngang so với cuối 2025 nhờ nguồn cung USD tích cực (từ kiều hối, FDI giải ngân và nâng 
hạng thị trường vốn) và chênh lệch lãi suất VND-USD dù có xu hướng thu hẹp nhưng 
vẫn duy trì trạng thái dương; và (2) lãi suất huy động sẽ duy trì ở vùng hiện tại trong 
phần còn lại của năm và kỳ vọng có thể giảm nhẹ nếu lạm phát được kiểm soát và 
NHNN sẽ có biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh khoản thị trường tích cực hơn.

## Document 291: Morning Report (2026-10-13)

Recent gains in global share 
markets stalled overnight, 
with losses across the US, 
Europe and much of Asia 
as investors paused after 
a strong run higher in risk 
assets. 
The FOMC’s Minutes 
reinforced the hawkish 
tone accompanying the 
Fed’s September rate hike, 
with most participants still 
viewing further tightening as 
appropriate and almost all 
seeing inflation risks tilted to 
the upside at the time of the 
meeting.
Treasury yields were mixed 
despite the hawkish Minutes, 
with strong demand at a 
10-year auction helping 
stabilise long-end yields 
and reinforcing a broader 
steepening of the curve. 
Oil prices remained elevated 
amid competing signals 
from the Middle East, with 
recovering crude flows offset 
by reports of increased 
attacks on shipping through 
the Strait of Hormuz.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• Share markets generally stalled across the globe. In the US,
stocks partially retraced gains from earlier in the week, with
the S&P 500 (–0.2%), Dow Jones (–0.7%) and NASDAQ
(–0.2%) all declining. Stocks fared worse across the pond,

with the Euro Stoxx 50 effectively unwinding all gains in
recent days (–1.5%), marked by chunky declines in Germany’s
DAX (–1.4%) and France’s CAC (–1.2%), alongside a larger
decline in London’s FTSE 100 (–0.8%). Stocks were also
weaker across much of Asia, falling in Tokyo (–0.9%), Hong
Kong (–0.6%) and Seoul (–2.0%). The ASX 200 only edged
slightly lower, down –0.1%, but futures markets are pointing
to a weaker open this morning.
•  The treasury yield curve steepened overnight. Gains in the

shorter-end saw 2-year yields fall 3bps to 4.77% – this was
despite a strong lift in consumer inflation expectations and
a hawkish FOMC Minutes, with market pricing for further
rate hikes from the FOMC instead softening at the margin.
Meanwhile, elevated yields at the long end drew very strong
demand at the latest 10-year auction, stabilising 10-year
yields at around 5.28%.
•  Bonds were more varied elsewhere, with Bunds bull
steepening amid a steady long-end, while Gilts bear

steepened amid a 6bp lift in the 10-year. ACGBs were
broadly steady during local trading, with yields inching only
slightly higher, though that gave way to gains in futures
trading overnight. Swaps markets continue to have around
6-7bps priced in for the RBA’s November meeting, while a
rate hike in December is seen at closer to 50/50 odds.
•  The USD bounced back overnight as the DXY rallied to an
intraday high of 102.5 before settling 0.4% higher at around

102.3. The Euro led the way lower, falling 0.6% versus the
greenback to just under USD1.12, back around its recent
lows. The Sterling shared a similar fate, down 0.5% toward
the USD1.321 level, remaining largely rangebound between
USD1.32-1.33 since late September. The Yen bucked the
wider trend, holding broadly steady. The Aussie dollar
meanwhile fell 0.3% to around USD0.696, unwinding gains
from earlier in the week.
•  Crude oil prices remain elevated as markets try to evaluate

the net impact of opposing forces – signs of recovering flows
of oil out of the Middle East is a positive, but reports that
Iran has increased the number of attacks on ships transiting
the Strait of Hormuz has kept a floor under prices. Brent was
up 0.5% to US$101.1/bbl overnight, still unable to break
sustainably under the US$100/bbl mark as of yet. Copper
prices lifted 0.4% to US$14,476 on the LME, gains being
driven by risks of tighter supply amid strikes at a major

Chilean mine, though the turn in risk sentiment overnight and
firmer USD capped gains. Gold prices meanwhile fell 1.3% to
around US$4,111/oz.
International Data
In the US, the FOMC’s Minutes  from the September meeting
reinforced the hawkish sentiment present at the time of the
decision to raise the fed funds rate by 25bps to a midpoint
of 3.875%. “All” participants supported the decision to raise
rates in September, and “most” saw further tightening by

year-end as appropriate. Underscoring that view was the fact
that “almost all” participants saw inflation risks as tilted to the
upside. Importantly, the assessment of the inflation outlook at
the time had already anticipated a 0.2ppt downward revision
to PCE inflation from the BEA’s change in methodology, which
ended up being 0.3ppt in the event.
The NY Fed’s measure of consumer inflation expectations
for the year ahead meanwhile surprised to the upside, rising

from 3.58% in for to 3.90% in September, marking the highest
result so far this year. Expectations also nudged 0.1ppt higher
to 3.3% at the three-year horizon, though they were stable at
3.0% at the five-year horizon.
Local Data
The Westpac Economics New Zealand team published its
September Retail Spending Pulse , which found that retail
spending remained resilient in the face of hgher fuel prices
(see here).

## Document 310: RBNZは追加利上げを実施したが、NZドル相場は上値の重い展開 ～景気は想定以上に底堅い一方、総選挙後の政策枠組み変更が新たな不透明要因に～

Source PDF: all report pdf/RBNZは追加利上げを実施したが、NZドル相場は上値の重い展開 ～景気は想定以上に底堅い一方、総選挙後の政策枠組み変更が新たな不透明要因に～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 9 月 17 日

1/4 
RBNZ は追加利上げを実施したが、NZ ドル相場は上値の重い展開 
～景気は想定以上に底堅い一方、総選挙後の政策枠組み変更が新たな不透明要因に～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは、 イラン情勢の悪化に伴う原油高を背景にインフレ率が加速し、 ４-６
月は前年比＋4.1％と RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の想定を上回った。これを受け、
RBNZ は９月会合で２会合連続となる利上げを実施した。 ただし、 コアインフレ率は目標レン
ジ内にとどまり、 雇用不安や賃金上昇圧力の弱さから基調的な物価上昇圧力は限定的である。
RBNZ も追加利上げの可能性を示しつつ、 その時期には大きな不確実性があるとの慎重姿勢を
示したため、ＮＺドル相場は調整の動きを強めている。 
○ ４-６月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前期比年率＋0.99％と減速したものの、RBNZ の予想を上回り、
前年同期比でも＋2.6％と３年ぶりの高い伸びとなった。 輸出や公的部門の投資が景気を支え
る一方、インフレや雇用不安によって個人消費は力強さを欠き、民間設備投資も弱含んでい
る。在庫調整が成長率を押し下げたことから、景気実態はＧＤＰの数字以上に底堅い可能性
もある。一方、「スーパー・エルニーニョ」に伴う異常気象が農林漁業などを下押ししてお
り、先行きの景気には不透明感が残る。 
○ 今後の金融政策とNZ ドル相場をみるうえでは、11 月７日の総選挙も重要となる。 最大野党 ・
労働党などは、RBNZ の政策目標に「持続可能な雇用の最大化」を再び加える方針を掲げてい
る。 足元では失業率が10 年ぶりの高水準となる一方、 コアインフレ率や賃金上昇率は比較的
落ち着いているため、政権交代に伴ってマンデートが変更されれば、利上げのハードルが高
まる可能性がある。このため、ＮＺドル相場は当面、追加利上げの行方に加え、総選挙の結
果やその後のRBNZ の政策枠組みを巡る議論にも左右されるとみられる。

### Page 2

2/4 
【RBNZは２会合連続で利上げ実施も､先行きの慎重姿勢が意識され､NZドル相場は調整】 
このところの金融市場においては、 ＮＺドル相場が調整の動きを強めている（図１）。 ニュージー
ランドでは、2025 年以降のインフレ率が加速して RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の定める目
標レンジ（１～３％）の上限を上回る伸
びで推移してきた。さらに、イラン情勢
の悪化による原油高はエネルギーイン
フレを招いており、４-６月のインフレ
率は前年比＋4.1％と2023 年10-12 月以
来の高い伸びに加速した。 この水準は
RBNZ が７月の定例 理事会で示した見通
し （前年比＋3.9％） を上回ったため、 そ
の後の金融市場では、RBNZ が９月の定例
理事会 で追加利上げに動くとの見方が
広がった。一方で、コアインフレ率は前
年比＋2.5％と目標レンジ内で推移するとともに、前期（同＋2.6％）からわずかに伸びが鈍化して
おり、エネルギー以外の物価は比較的落ち着いている。これは、雇用不安が根強く賃金上昇圧力が
高まりにくく、価格転嫁の動きが広がりにくいことが影響している可能性がある。 
RBNZ は９月１～２日に開催した定例理事会において、前述した金融市場の予想通り利上げ実施
を決定した（注１）。RBNZ による利上げは２会合連続であり、金融引き締めの動きを着実に進めて
おり、通常であればＮＺドル相場を下支えすることが期待される。しかし、会合後に公表された声
明文では、 先行きの政策運営について、 追加利上げに対して慎重姿勢を強めている様子がうかがえ
た。会合後に記者会見に臨んだ RBNZ のブレマン総裁も、先行きの政策運営について、追加利上げ
に動く可能性は高いとの見方を示す一方、事前に示した政策経路に固執することはせず、 利上げの
タイミングは極めて不確実との考えをみせた。

注１ ９月２日付レポート「RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654752.html） 
図 1 NZ ドル相場(対米ドル､日本円)の推移

### Page 3

3/4 
【景気は鈍化したが､RBNZの想定を上回る｡ただし､先行きの景気には不透明要因が山積】 
こうしたなか、景気動向が先行きの金融政策の方向性を左右すると見込まれるなか、 ４-６月の実
質ＧＤＰ成長率は前期比年率＋ 0.99％
と前期 （同＋3.74％） から鈍化して４四
半期ぶりの低い伸びとなった（図２）。
しかし、RBNZ は９月の定例理事会で示
した見通し （前期比＋0.0％） を上回り、
景気に対するリスク懸念が和らぐ可能
性がある。中期的な基調を示す前年同期
比ベースの成長率は＋2.6％と前期（同
＋1.7％）から加速して３年ぶりの高い
伸びとなるなど、 足元の景気は底堅い動
きをみせている。 
需要項目別の動きをみると、年明け直後にかけてはいわゆる「トランプ関税」の本格発動を前に
した駆け込みが輸出を押し上げた。その後は、米連邦最高裁は相互関税を違法とする判決を下し、
直後に米国政府は相互関税を停止し代替関税を導入したことで、 実質的に関税率は引き下げられた。
こうした動きも追い風に輸出は堅調に推移しており、景気をけん引している。一方で、インフレ加
速による実質購買力の下押しに加え、雇用不安も重なり、個人消費は頭打ちの動きを強めている。
また、RBNZ による利上げ実施が意識される形で企業部門の設備投資意欲が後退する一方、 防衛関連
をはじめとする公的部門による投資拡大の動きは固定資本投資を押し上げており、内需は公的需要
への依存を強めている。 なお、 在庫投資による成長率寄与度は前期比年率ベースで▲1.62pt と３四
半期ぶりのマイナスになったと試算され、 在庫調整が進んでいることが確認されており、 景気実態
は数字に比べて良好である可能性が考えられる。 
分野別の生産動向についても、 エネルギーインフレや供給懸念が足かせとなることが懸念された
ものの、輸出の堅調さを反映して製造業の生産は緩やかな拡大が続いている。さらに、公的部門を
中心とする設備投資の動きは建設業の生産を押し上げている。 なお、 個人消費は力強さを欠いてい
るにもかかわらず、 世界的な金融取引の活況を追い風に、 金融関連を中心とするサービス業の生産
に底堅い動きもみられる。 その一方、 豪雨による洪水や土砂崩れの発生といった異常気象が影響す
る形で、農林漁業や鉱業の生産にいずれも下押し圧力がかかり、景気の足を引っ張っている。2026
年は太平洋の赤道付近で海水温が平年に比べて高くなるエルニーニョ現象が発生しており、 今回は
平年からの海水温上昇幅が２℃以上に達する「スーパー・エルニーニョ」となっている。足元では
すでに観測史上最強の水準となっているうえ、 事態が長期化するとの見方も出ている。過去のエル
ニーニョ現象では、北島および南島の北部・東部で乾燥による干ばつ・山火事が、南島の西部・南
部では大雨による洪水・土砂崩れが発生し、主力産業の農業・畜産業に深刻な悪影響が出た。この
ため、先行きの景気に対する不透明感は高まっている可能性がある。

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
【NZドル相場を巡っては､総選挙後のRBNZのマンデートの行方が影響する可能性に注意】 
前述したように、４-６月のＧＤＰが RBNZ の想定を上回ったことは、先行きの政策運営を巡って
景気に対する懸念が後退する可能性を示唆している。 このため、RBNZ にとっては追加利上げ実施の
ハードルが低下する可能性が考えられる。金融政策を取り巻く環境をみるうえでは、11 月７日に実
施される代議院総選挙の動向にも注意
が必要である （注２） 。 背景には、RBNZ の
政策運営がここ数年、政治的な争点とな
ってきたことがある。最大野党・労働党
などはRBNZ のマンデート （政策目標） の
変更を公約に掲げており、政権交代が起
これば、持続可能な雇用の最大化が再び
加えられると見込まれる。雇用環境を巡
っては、直近４-６月の失業率が10 年ぶ
りの高水準となったことが確認されて
いる （図３）。前述したように、直近のコ
アインフレ率は RBNZ の目標レンジ内で推移しているうえ、賃金上昇率はインフレ率を下回る伸び
となるなど物価に与える影響が依然として軽微なものにとどまるため、 利上げのハードルが高まる
ことも予想される。 
RBNZ は政策決定が総選挙に左右されない姿勢を明確にしている。10 月28 日の次回理事会は総選
挙前に開催されるうえ、 現行のマンデートが中期的な物価安定を政策目標としていることを勘案す
れば、 選挙そのものが政策判断に直接影響する可能性は低い。 一方、12 月９日に予定される理事会
は総選挙後に開催されるため、 その時点の政治情勢に加え、RBNZ のマンデート見直しを巡る議論が
金融政策の先行きに対する市場の見方に影響する可能性がある。このため、 先行きのＮＺドル相場
の行方を巡っては、総選挙の結果と、その後の RBNZ の政策枠組みに関する議論に左右される可能
性にも注意が必要である。 
以 上

## Document 325: The Bond Yield Spectrum

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Bond Yield Spectrum.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: The Bond Yield Spectrum
Date:    5 hours ago
The Bond Yield Spectrum

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
The Bond Yield Spectrum
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 7 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Adam Kring / Unsplash

I. From The Good To The Bad To The Ugly
The spectrum of explanations for the rise in bond yields worldwide
includes relatively benign ones to downright dangerous ones (chart). On
the good side of the spectrum, better-than-expected economic growth has
driven US bond yields up to levels that are back to normal. On the ugly
side, soaring bond yields in France are signaling a looming debt crisis. In
between are bad scenarios that explain the synchronized rise in global

### Page 2

bond yields. These include the inflationary energy shock attributable to the
war in the Middle East and the unwinding of the yen-carry trade.
Let's have a closer look at the good, the bad, and the ugly:
(1) R-star rising. R-star, or the neutral interest rate, is the real interest
rate consistent with low unemployment and price stability. It balances the
supply of savings with demand for investment. Three forces are pushing it
higher.
First, demand for capital is booming along with AI-related capital

spending. Hyperscaler capex is expected to reach $750-$800 billion this
year and $1.2 trillion next. Total US corporate bond issuance over the past
12 months through August was a record $3.0 trillion, including $1.4 trillion
and $1.6 trillion issued by nonfinancial and financial corporations,
respectively (chart).

### Page 3

At the same time, US Treasury borrowing totaled $2.1 trillion over the past
12 months through September, including $1.3 trillion in notes and bonds
(chart).
Second, national savings faces demographic headwinds as retiring Baby
Boomers stop saving and draw down their net worth (chart).

### Page 4

Third, stronger productivity growth may be raising the return on capital
and the economy’s sustainable growth rate (chart).
The Fed's 175bps cuts in the federal funds rate since September 2024
have been completely offset by a comparable increase in the 10-year
Treasury bond yield since then. Even at the start of this year, most Fed
officials believed that the FFR was modestly restrictive, i.e., that it
exceeded the neutral rate. In September, they acknowledged their error by

hiking the FFR by 25 basis points, as Fed Chair Kevin Warsh said they
had removed a "dose of accommodation," implying that the FFR is
actually below the neutral rate!

### Page 5

(2) Energy shock. Moving toward the middle of the spectrum are higher-
for-longer oil prices. With US-Iran talks stalled and the US increasing its
military assets, the risk of renewed escalation remains high. The longer
energy costs stay elevated, the greater the risk they'll boost core inflation,
forcing a more aggressive Fed response. If crude oil prices fall because
more oil is getting through the Strait of Hormuz, diesel prices might remain
elevated (chart).

(3) Unwinding yen-carry trade. The yen-carry trade was based on ultra-
low Japanese interest rates and a stable or weak yen. Now the BOJ is
raising interest rates and the yen may be bottoming (charts). This is
forcing traders to liquidate bond holdings previously financed with cheap
Japanese money.

### Page 6

(4) Debt crisis. At the far end of the spectrum is the "Revenge of the
Bond Vigilantes." Between the Great Financial Crisis and the Great Virus
Crisis, major central banks' ultra-easy policies enabled governments to
run large deficits. The Bond Vigilantes were powerless. But now they are
more powerful than ever because government debt is at record highs.
Central banks have been forced to raise their policy rates as inflation has
been more troublesome in recent years. Bond yields have soared this

year worldwide as inflation rebounded and fiscal-risk premiums have
risen.
The danger is a self-reinforcing debt spiral: Higher yields raise interest
costs, widen deficits, require more borrowing, and push yields higher still
(chart). And, of course, higher yields increase the risk of causing a
recession, which would exacerbate any debt crisis.

### Page 7

In our view, the US should remain in the higher R-star end of the
spectrum. We would start to worry about the debt-crisis danger zone if the
US bond yield rises above nominal GDP growth (chart).
In France, on the other hand, the Bond Vigilantes are pushing the 10-year
government bond yield well above nominal GDP growth (chart). The
French could be toast if yields continue to soar.
II. The Fed Minutes
The latest Fed minutes paint a familiar picture: a resilient economy, a firm

labor market, and inflation that remains too high. We drew three key
takeaways from the minutes of the Fed’s September 15-16 meeting.

### Page 8

(1) Restrictiveness. Several participants said the current policy rate was
not restrictive or only mildly restrictive, while a couple explicitly raised their
estimates of the neutral federal funds rate. That fits neatly with our R-star
story.
(2) Macro backdrop. The Fed saw a resilient economy: real GDP is
expanding at a solid pace, consumer spending has firmed, AI investment
remains robust, and the labor market is close to maximum employment. At

the same time, inflation remains elevated and risks were still skewed to
the upside.
(3) Rate outlook. All participants backed September’s 25bps hike, and
most judged that another increase would likely be appropriate by year-
end.
💡
Join the discussion with Ed below! To leave comments or questions,
log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

clause.

## Document 329: The hobby economy and the price of fun Consumers are paying more for fun as Millennials and Gen Z reshape how they spend their leisure time. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/The hobby economy and the price of fun Consumers are paying more for fun as Millennials and Gen Z reshape how they spend their leisure time. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13020137     1

Economy 
The hobby economy and the price of fun 
23 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumers are spending more on hobbies. Hobby spending rose 7.9% year-over-year (YoY) in August, but this was more than 
double the pace of transaction growth, suggesting the reemergence of "funflation" in hobbies, according to Bank of America 
card data. 
• Older Millennials are the biggest hobby spenders, according to Bank of America card data, despite having the least amount of 
leisure time of any generation. They also have the highest share of the population with hobby spending, though this likely 
reflects both personal interests and spending on their kids. 
• Gen Z's hobby habits are evolving. While hobby purchases have slowed sharply, younger consumers continue to devote 
significant leisure time to gaming, with over a quarter of Gen Z having some type of online or video game spending, according 
to Bank of America card data.

Spending grows over twice as fast as transactions in August 
Bank of America card data suggests we’re seeing some “funflation” (the rising price of fun) in hobbies – defined here as arts and 
crafts stores, hobby shops and outdoor recreation service providers and retailers that specialize in activities like hiking, camping, 
skiing and scuba diving (see Methodology).  
Using our definition, spending on hobbies grew 7.9% year-over-year (YoY) in August 2026. This was over twice as fast as 
transaction growth, which was up a solid 3.4% YoY (Exhibit 1). This is the opposite of last August, when transactions outpaced 
spending by nearly a percentage point. It’s also a stark contrast from 2023, when spending fell nearly 4% YoY and transactions 
were up almost 1%.  
Exhibit 1: Spending grew at twice the rate of transactions in August 2026 
Spending and transaction growth per household on hobbies (three-month moving average, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking across a longer period, we see that spending growth also outpaced that of transactions throughout the pandemic 
(Exhibit 2). Transactions began declining in 2021, with spending following suit in mid-2022. In fact, spending declined faster 
than transactions from 2023 until late 2024, with both improving the following year.

In our view, it’s likely that people splurged on hobbies amid social distancing during the pandemic, then gravitated back toward 
pricier alternatives like travel throughout 2022 and 2023. However, last year there may have been some rotation back to less 
expensive leisure like hobbies as after-tax wage growth slowed considerably (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling). 
Bank of America card data suggests there’s been a recent acceleration in hobby spending in the past few months, possibly as 
some consumers balance shift away from travel due to higher prices from rising fuel costs. 
Exhibit 2: Spending growth outpaces transaction growth, while the opposite was true from mid-2022 until the end of 2024 
Spending and transaction growth per household on hobbies (three-month moving average, YoY%) and average spending per transaction growth per household

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Older Millennials claim the hobbies crown, while Gen Z lags behind 
Looking across generations, Bank of America card data suggests that older Millennials spend the most per customer on hobbies, 
followed closely by Baby Boomers and Gen X, while Gen Z spends the least (Exhibit 3).  
Exhibit 3: Older Millennials spend the most on hobbies, followed by Baby Boomers and Gen X 
Average spending per customer by age generation (three-month moving average to August 2026, index Gen Z = 100)

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Older Millennials also boast the largest share of their population with hobby spending (Exhibit 4 and Exhibit 5). In our view, it’s 
likely because this group is most likely to have young children. Which may mean older Millennials not only spend on their own 
hobbies but on their kids’ as well.

The idea that older Millennials are likely spending on themselves and their kids is supported by data from the American Time Use 
Survey (ATUS) conducted by the US Census Bureau. That survey suggests that older Millennials have the least amount of leisure 
time, with only around four hours and fifteen minutes on average per day (Exhibit 6). Meanwhile, the two groups between ages 
“15 and 24” and “55 and 64” have nearly five and a half hours per day, with those 65 and older boasting over seven hours of 
leisure time per day.   
On the other hand, younger Millennials and Gen Z have the lowest average spending, but a higher share of their population with 
hobby outlays. To us, this suggests that these generations are likely gravitating most of their spending toward less expensive 
hobbies like arts, crafts and board games.

Gen Z consumers slow hobby purchases… 
Additionally, while younger and older Millennials as well as Gen X appear to be ramping up hobby spending, Baby Boomers and 
Gen Z are slowing theirs (Exhibit 7). Notably though, Gen Z’s slowdown in purchases has been stark. Transaction growth was near 
zero in August 2026, a dramatic slowdown compared to the nearly 16% YoY increase the prior year, according to Bank of 
America card data (Exhibit 8).

This slowdown was driven by a significant decrease in spending on outdoor recreation, offsetting increases in arts and crafts 
retailers and hobby shops. In our view, the prior year’s transactions may have been boosted by purchases of longer-lasting items, 
such as hiking boots, which don’t need to be bought as frequently. It’s also possible that this reflects a shift to more “granny 
core” activities like knitting, sewing, or baking (arts and crafts). 
…and ramp up their gaming spend 
Additionally, some transactions at hobby shops may require less frequent purchases like board games or tabletop role-playing 
games. The social component to these activities can’t be overlooked as a factor. In fact, over a quarter of Gen Z said they feel a 
sense of belonging to a streaming or gaming community, according to a Bank of America survey (Exhibit 9). This is almost two 
and half times as high as younger Millennials and over eight times the rate of Baby Boomers.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Hobby spending includes spending across all payment vehicles at arts and crafts stores, hobby shops, ski resorts, scuba retailers and rentals, 
adventure activities, educational toys, outdoor retailers and some toy stores. Not included in this analysis are some hobbies like golf or travel, 
due to further analysis in other pieces. Also not included are more digital forms of entertainment like video games, though this category is 
included as a separate analysis.   
Includes a reference to Bank of America’s Navigator Survey’s question “Outside of your family, in which places or groups do you currently feel a 
sense of community or belonging? (Select all that apply).”  The survey was conducted between January 16 and 22, 2026 and included 2,041 
participants.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. Our Gen Z analysis only includes 
spending by people that are 18 years or older and likely reflects the trends from the members of this generation who are living on their own.  
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 8

8 23 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Largan Precision
Core weakness outweighs progress in AI optics
CPO progress is more tangible but substantial success is already priced in
We maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target price as erosion in the core smartphone
business outweighs CPO progress. The Q326 call confirmed that Largan's fiber array has
passed customer qualification although its customer qualified another vendor's prism
micro-lens assembly (PMLA) that Largan would now buy in. If Largan qualifies the full

FAU plus carrier assembly for 2H27, content rises but with more pass-through cost and
less margin upside. We roll our valuation to 2028E, the first full year of CPO production,
though 25% below the NT$6,660 close on October 8.
Full FAU assembly would lift content, but with more pass-through
We believe Largan's FA customer is one of two  module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27
program, which we estimate could reach 40% share. The program is still subject to

successful yield ramp and could move to metalens in 2028, which would reopen supplier
qualification. Assembling the full FAU could lift Largan's ASP to US$125-150 leveraging
its precision alignment and automation but the PMLA, V-groove and carrier parts would
be pass through that cuts into margin leverage. We expand our FAU TAM analysis
(Figure 9S u m m a r y  o f  F A U  d r i v e r s :  1 2 . 3 m n / 3 5 . 6 m n  b a s e  c a s e  i n  2 0 2 8 - 2 0 3 0 ) with FAU market in 2028/30 expanding to 12.3mn/35.6mn units, with Largan

base case set at 25% of the TAM.
Core smartphone business stays soft into 2027
Q326 sales of NT$15.7bn, +15% QoQ/-11% YoY missedUBSe/Street +36%/+33% on
weaker flagship smartphone demand. GMs fell 7.2ppts QoQ to 42.2%, lowest in 13
years on renewable energy costs and low variable-aperture yields, dropping OpM to 17
year lows at 30.3% and EPS to NT$39.6, below UBS/street NT$51.50/NT$49.69. We cut
Q4 to NT$16.7bn +7% QoQ, 11% below consensus as its customer trimmed orders

after a pricier iPhone 18 launch and possible lag to Genius which is picking up periscope
share and outgrew in Q3 (+1% YoY vs. -11% YoY). We cut 2027 from NT$77bn to NT
$67bn, before a recovery to NT$79bn in 2028 with the next periscope upgrade. We see
GM recovering to 47.5%/48.9% in 2027-28 as yields improve and energy costs pass
through in 2028 pricing.
Maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target
We cut 2026E/27E EPS by 14%/16% to NT$160/NT$180 and introduce 2028E EPS of

NT$220 for the core smartphone business. We value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x
2028E ex-CPO EPS of NT$220, the middle of Largan's 13–18x historical range) and CPO
option at NT$1,700 or US$7bn (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35, near the
top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-unit, US
$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. That credits it with
yields and margins it still needs to prove in a quarter when yields and energy costs cut

7.2ppts from its lens margin. At ~NT$6,660, the shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO
EPS, about twice our base case.

Core smartphone lens seeing weakness with declining
margins
Q326 sales was reported light at NT$15.7bn, +15% QoQ missing UBSe/Street +36%/
+33% amid weaker flagship smartphone demand. GMs fell 720bp to 42.2%, lowest in
13 years on renewable energy costs and low variable-aperture yields, dropping OpM to
17 year lows at 30.3% and EPS to NT$39.6, below UBS/street NT$51.50/NT$49.69.
Renewable-energy costs are structural under Largan's long-term power commitments

and rise with Apple's share of the mix; with 2027 pricing set, pass-through is more likely
in 2028.
We cut Q4 to NT$16.7bn +7% QoQ, 11% below consensus as its customer trimmed
orders after a pricier iPhone 18 launch and possible lag to Genius which is picking up
periscope share and outgrew in Q3 (flat vs. -11% YoY).  October revenue should be flat
MoM and November lower as the key customer has shortened its forecast after cutting
flagship orders in September. The spring-2027 model starts shipping in October but will

not offset the flagship decline.

CPO fiber array qualified, but rising pass-through as it upgrades to FAU
production
Largan's fiber array (FA) has passed qualification with a volume customer, and
management expects mass-production capacity by mid-2027 in a newly completed fab
dedicated to FA/FAU, using in-house equipment. The company's next step is to produce
the full detachable FAU mounted on a carrier, a board with three alignment beams that
sits on the optical engine's receptor. Largan can build FAUs now and says the customer

### Page 3

Largan Precision 12 October 2026 ab 3
would prefer it to supply the full assembly, but qualification is pending with the
customer preferring FAU and carrier sampled separately.
Even as its potential to supply the full FAU improve, Largan will buy in most of the
content. The customer has qualified another PMLA vendor and prefers semiconductor-
made optics; V-grooves and carrier beams and boards are outsourced and the customer
does not want Largan to make MT ferrules. Management described its role as closer to a

contractor and said margins hinge on yields, as the FA carries a high ASP but also a large
bill of materials.  Supplying the complete FAU could raise billed content, but a larger
purchased bill of materials also dilutes the relationship between revenue and value
added. Our full-FAU ASP assumption requires Largan to capture that invoice scope.
Next year's product is grating-coupled and works for both CPO and NPO while edge-
coupled designs are being explored with the customer. The current product does not go

directly to end customers, but management expects to work directly with end customers
on next-generation designs (likely 2028) as it progresses toward multi-row FAs.
To ramp production, Largan has bought several land parcels and will build new fabs from
scratch, with construction finishing around 2030. It may outsource some capacity in the
interim and said it cannot yet be sure capacity will meet demand.
Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes and even mix
of these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s
ASICs using 6.4Tb/s optical engines.

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030, 55% of 1.1mn 204.8T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.
